Whatever is passion, being passionate, inclination, favoring, delight, delight in passion, passion of mind, desire, confusion, clinging, greed, thick greed, attachment, mud, stickiness, fever, deceit, what gives birth, what ties, what weaves, what nets, what flows, what sticks, what spreads, what pulls along, second wish, plan, stream of becoming, forest, thicket, entanglement, affection, expectation, bond, hope, wishing, state of wishing, hope for visible form, hope for sound, hope for smell, hope for taste, hope for touch, hope for gain, hope for wealth, hope for children, hope for life, muttering, repeated muttering, intense muttering, muttering, state of muttering, greediness, being greedy, state of being greedy, being bent on gain, wanting what is good improperly, wrong passion, uneven greed, bending toward, wishing, longing, aspiring, craving for sense pleasures, craving for becoming, craving for non-becoming, craving for visible form, craving for sound, craving for smell, craving for taste, craving for touch, craving for mental objects, flood, yoke, knot, grasping, obstruction, hindrance, covering, bond, stain, underlying tendency, outbreak, creeper, dissatisfaction, root of suffering, source of suffering, origin of suffering, snare of Māra, bait of Māra, domain of Māra, river of craving, net of craving, rope of craving, ocean of craving, coveting, greed as a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called the knot of coveting. ^1-1141

1142. What is the knot of ill-will? "He did me wrong" — hatred arises; "he does me wrong" — hatred arises; "he will do me wrong" — hatred arises; "he did wrong to what is dear and pleasing to me"…pe… "he does wrong"…pe… "he will do wrong" — hatred arises; "he did good to what is disliked and displeasing to me"…pe… "he does good"…pe… "he will do good" — hatred arises; or hatred arises where there is no reason. Whatever hatred of this kind in the mind — hatred, resistance, opposing, anger, angry state, full anger, hatred, hateful state, full hatred, crookedness of mind, ill-will of mind, hatred, anger, being angry, state of being angry, hatred, hating, state of hating, ill-will, injuring, state of injuring, opposition, resisting, fierceness, being like an enemy, displeasure of mind — this is called the knot of ill-will. ^1-1142

1143. What is the knot of taking virtue and observances as sufficient? The view of outside ascetics and brahmins that "purity comes from virtue, purity comes from observance, purity comes from virtue and observance"; whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view, deep view, view as a thorn, view as distortion, view as agitation, thing that ties the mind down — view, grasping, taking up, settling into, holding to, wrong path, wrong way, wrongness, sectarian standpoint, upside-down grasping — this is called the knot of taking virtue and observances as sufficient. ^1-1143

1144. What is the knot of insistence that this alone is true? "The world is eternal — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the world is not eternal — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the world is finite — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the world is infinite — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the soul is the same as the body — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the soul is one thing and the body another — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the Tathāgata exists after death — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the Tathāgata does not exist after death — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness," or "the Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death — this alone is true, anything else is foolishness"; whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view, deep view, view as a thorn, view as distortion, view as agitation, thing that ties the mind down — view, grasping, taking up, settling into, holding to, wrong path, wrong way, wrongness, sectarian standpoint, upside-down grasping — this is called the knot of insistence that this alone is true. Except the knot of taking virtue and observances as sufficient, all wrong view is the knot of insistence that this alone is true.
These things are knots. ^1-1144

1145. Which things are not knots? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not knots. ^1-1145

1146. Which things can knot? Things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind defiled at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can knot. ^1-1146

1147. Which things cannot knot? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things cannot knot. ^1-1147

1148. Which things are associated with knots? Whatever things are associated with those knots — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with knots. ^1-1148

1149. Which things are dissociated from knots? Whatever things are dissociated from those knots — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from knots. ^1-1149

1150. Which things are both knots and can knot? Those very knots are both knots and can knot. ^1-1150

1151. Which things can knot but are not knots? Except those knots, the remaining defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can knot but are not knots. ^1-1151

1152. Which things are both knots and associated with knots? The knot of taking virtue and observances as sufficient is both a knot and associated with the knot of coveting as knot; the knot of coveting is both a knot and associated with the knot of taking virtue and observances as sufficient as knot; the knot of insistence that this alone is true is both a knot and associated with the knot of coveting as knot; the knot of coveting is both a knot and associated with the knot of insistence that this alone is true as knot — these things are both knots and associated with knots. ^1-1152

1153. Which things are associated with knots but are not knots? Except those knots, aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with knots but are not knots. ^1-1153

1154. Which things are dissociated from knots but can knot? Whatever things are dissociated from those knots but can knot — defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are dissociated from knots but can knot. ^1-1154

1155. Which things are dissociated from knots and cannot knot? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from knots and cannot knot. ^1-1155

##### Group on Floods ^1-3-2-6-0

1156. Which things are floods? Four floods…pe… these things are dissociated from floods but can flood. ^1-1156

##### Group on Yokes ^1-3-2-7-0

1157. Which things are yokes? Four yokes…pe… these things are dissociated from yokes but can yoke. ^1-1157

##### Group on Hindrances ^1-3-2-8-0

1158. Which things block the mind? Six things that block the mind — hindrance of desire for sense pleasures, hindrance of ill-will, thing that blocks the mind — stiffness and sleepiness, hindrance of restlessness and remorse, hindrance of doubt, hindrance of ignorance. ^1-1158

1159. What is the thing that blocks the mind — desire for sense pleasures? Whatever desire for sense pleasures, passion for sense pleasures, delight in sense pleasures, craving for sense pleasures, affection for sense pleasures, intensity for sense pleasures, confusion for sense pleasures, clinging to sense pleasures — this is called the thing that blocks the mind — desire for sense pleasures. ^1-1159

1160. What is the thing that blocks the mind — ill-will? "He did me wrong" — hatred arises; "he does me wrong" — hatred arises; "he will do me wrong" — hatred arises; "he did wrong to what is dear and pleasing to me"…pe… "he does wrong"…pe… "he will do wrong" — hatred arises; "he did good to what is disliked and displeasing to me"…pe… "he does good"…pe… "he will do good" — hatred arises; or hatred arises where there is no reason. Whatever hatred of this kind in the mind — hatred, resistance, opposing, anger, angry state, full anger, hatred, hateful state, full hatred, crookedness of mind, ill-will of mind, hatred, anger, being angry, state of being angry, hatred, hating, state of hating, ill-will, injuring, state of injuring, opposition, resisting, fierceness, being like an enemy, displeasure of mind — this is called the thing that blocks the mind — ill-will. ^1-1160

1161. What is the thing that blocks the mind — stiffness and sloth? There is stiffness; there is sleepiness. ^1-1161

1162. What is stiffness? Whatever unfitness and unworkability of mind, sinking, settling, being sunk, settling, settled state, stiffness, stiffening, stiff state of mind — this is called stiffness. ^1-1162

1163. What is sloth? Whatever unfitness and unworkability of the body, bending down, bending further down, inward closing, sloth, sleep, drowsiness, sleeping, state of sleeping. This stiffness and this sloth — this is called the thing that blocks the mind — stiffness and sloth. ^1-1163

1164. What is the thing that blocks the mind — restlessness and remorse? There is restlessness; there is remorse. ^1-1164

1165. What is restlessness? Whatever restlessness, unsettledness, scattering, scattered state of mind — this is called restlessness. ^1-1165

1166. What is remorse? Taking the improper as proper, taking the proper as improper, taking the blameable as blameless, taking the blameless as blameable. Whatever remorse of this kind — remorse, remorsing, remorseful state, regret of mind, crookedness of mind. This restlessness and this remorse — this is called the thing that blocks the mind — restlessness and remorse. ^1-1166

1167. What is the thing that blocks the mind — doubt? Doubt and wavering about the Teacher, about the teaching, about the community, about the training, about the past, about the future, about past and future together, about things that arise dependently with conditions as basis. Whatever wavering of this kind — doubting, state of doubting, confusion, doubt, being of two minds, double path, uncertainty, not taking one side, hesitation, trembling, not going all the way through, stiffness of mind, crookedness of mind — this is called the thing that blocks the mind — doubt. ^1-1167

1168. What is the thing that blocks the mind — ignorance? Not knowing suffering, not knowing the origin of suffering, not knowing the cessation of suffering, not knowing the way leading to the cessation of suffering, not knowing the past, not knowing the future, not knowing past and future together, not knowing things that arise dependently with conditions as basis; whatever not-knowing of this kind — ignorance, not seeing, not understanding, not penetrating, not realizing, not taking in, not going all the way through, not looking at, not reviewing, not looking back, dullness, foolishness, lack of clear comprehension, delusion, full delusion, complete delusion, ignorance, flood of ignorance, yoke of ignorance, underlying tendency of ignorance, outbreak of ignorance, banner of ignorance, delusion as a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called the thing that blocks the mind — ignorance.
These things are hindrances. ^1-1168

1169. Which things do not block the mind? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things do not block the mind. ^1-1169

1170. Which things can hinder? Things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind defiled at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can hinder. ^1-1170

1171. Which things cannot hinder? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things cannot hinder. ^1-1171

1172. Which things are associated with things that block the mind? Whatever things are associated with those hindrances — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with things that block the mind. ^1-1172

1173. Which things are dissociated from things that block the mind? Whatever things are dissociated from those hindrances — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from things that block the mind. ^1-1173

1174. Which things are both things that block the mind and can hinder? Those very things that block the mind are both things that block the mind and can hinder. ^1-1174

1175. Which things can hinder but are not hindrances? Except those things that block the mind, the remaining defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can hinder but are not hindrances. ^1-1175

1176. Which things are both things that block the mind and associated with things that block the mind? The hindrance of desire for sense pleasures is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ignorance as hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of desire for sense pleasures as hindrance; the hindrance of ill-will is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ignorance as hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ill-will as hindrance; the thing that blocks the mind — stiffness and sleepiness is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ignorance as hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with the thing that blocks the mind — stiffness and sleepiness as hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ignorance as hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of restlessness as hindrance; the hindrance of remorse is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ignorance as hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of remorse as hindrance; the hindrance of doubt is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ignorance as hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of doubt as hindrance; the hindrance of desire for sense pleasures is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of restlessness as hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of desire for sense pleasures as hindrance; the hindrance of ill-will is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of restlessness as hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ill-will as hindrance; the thing that blocks the mind — stiffness and sleepiness is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of restlessness as hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with the thing that blocks the mind — stiffness and sleepiness as hindrance; the hindrance of remorse is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of restlessness as hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of remorse as hindrance; the hindrance of doubt is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of restlessness as hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of doubt as hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of restlessness as hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with the hindrance of ignorance as hindrance — these things are both things that block the mind and associated with things that block the mind. ^1-1176

1177. Which things are associated with things that block the mind but are not hindrances? Except those things that block the mind, aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with things that block the mind but are not hindrances. ^1-1177

1178. Which things are dissociated from things that block the mind but can hinder? Whatever things are dissociated from those hindrances but can hinder — defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are dissociated from things that block the mind but can hinder. ^1-1178

1179. Which things are dissociated from things that block the mind and cannot hinder? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from things that block the mind and cannot hinder. ^1-1179

##### Group on Misapprehension ^1-3-2-9-0

1180. Which things are misapprehensions? Misapprehension through view. ^1-1180

1181. What is misapprehension through view? "The world is eternal," or "the world is not eternal," or "the world is finite," or "the world is infinite," or "the soul is the same as the body," or "the soul is one thing and the body another," or "the Tathāgata exists after death," or "the Tathāgata does not exist after death," or "the Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death," or "the Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death"; whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view, deep view, view as a thorn, view as distortion, view as agitation, thing that ties the mind down — view, grasping, taking up, settling into, holding to, wrong path, wrong way, wrongness, sectarian standpoint, upside-down grasping — this is called misapprehension through view. All wrong view is misapprehension through view.
These things are misapprehensions. ^1-1181

1182. Which things are not misapprehensions? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not misapprehensions. ^1-1182

1183. Which things can be misapprehended? Things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind defiled at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can be misapprehended. ^1-1183

1184. Which things cannot be misapprehended? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things cannot be misapprehended. ^1-1184

1185. Which things are associated with misapprehension? Whatever things are associated with those misapprehensions — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with misapprehension. ^1-1185

1186. Which things are dissociated from misapprehension? Whatever things are dissociated from those misapprehensions — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from misapprehension. ^1-1186

1187. Which things are both misapprehensions and can be misapprehended? That very misapprehension is both misapprehension and can be misapprehended. ^1-1187

1188. Which things can be misapprehended but are not misapprehensions? Except that misapprehension, the remaining defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can be misapprehended but are not misapprehensions. ^1-1188

1189. Which things are dissociated from misapprehension but can be misapprehended? Whatever things are dissociated from those misapprehensions but can be misapprehended — defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are dissociated from misapprehension but can be misapprehended. ^1-1189

1190. Which things are dissociated from misapprehension and cannot be misapprehended? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from misapprehension and cannot be misapprehended. ^1-1190

##### Large Inner Pairs ^1-3-2-10-0

1191. Which things have an object? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, aggregate of consciousness — these things have an object. ^1-1191

1192. Which things are without an object? All materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are without an object. ^1-1192

1193. Which things are minds? Eye consciousness, ear consciousness, nose consciousness, tongue consciousness, body consciousness, mind-element, mind-consciousness-element — these things are minds. ^1-1193

1194. Which things are not minds? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not minds. ^1-1194

1195. Which things are mental qualities? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions — these things are mental qualities. ^1-1195

1196. Which things are not mental qualities? The mind, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not mental qualities. ^1-1196

1197. Which things are associated with the mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions — these things are associated with the mind. ^1-1197

1198. Which things are dissociated from the mind? All materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from the mind. The mind should not be said to be either associated with the mind or dissociated from the mind. ^1-1198

1199. Which things are mixed together with the mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions — these things are mixed together with the mind. ^1-1199

1200. Which things are not mixed together with the mind? All materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not mixed together with the mind. The mind should not be said to be either mixed together with the mind or not mixed together with the mind. ^1-1200

1201. Which things are arisen from the mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions; bodily expression, verbal expression; and whatever other materiality there is mind-born, with mind as cause, arisen from the mind — visible-form base, sound base, smell base, taste base, touch base, space element, water element, lightness of materiality, softness of materiality, fitness for use of materiality, growth of materiality, continuity of materiality, nutriment taken in lumps — these things are arisen from the mind. ^1-1201

1202. Which things are not arisen from the mind? The mind, the remaining materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not arisen from the mind. ^1-1202

1203. Which things are born together with the mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, bodily expression, verbal expression — these things are born together with the mind. ^1-1203

1204. Which things are not born together with the mind? The mind, the remaining materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not born together with the mind. ^1-1204

1205. Which things follow along with the mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, bodily expression, verbal expression — these things follow along with the mind. ^1-1205

1206. Which things do not follow along with the mind? The mind, the remaining materiality, the unconditioned element — these things do not follow along with the mind. ^1-1206

1207. Which things are arisen from what is mixed together with the mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions — these things are arisen from what is mixed together with the mind. ^1-1207

1208. Which things are not arisen from what is mixed together with the mind? The mind, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not arisen from what is mixed together with the mind. ^1-1208

1209. Which things are born together with what is arisen from what is mixed together with the mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions — these things are born together with what is arisen from what is mixed together with the mind. ^1-1209

1210. Which things are not born together with what is arisen from what is mixed together with the mind? The mind, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not born together with what is arisen from what is mixed together with the mind. ^1-1210

1211. Which things follow along with what is arisen from what is mixed together with the mind? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions — these things follow along with what is arisen from what is mixed together with the mind. ^1-1211

1212. Which things do not follow along with what is arisen from what is mixed together with the mind? The mind, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things do not follow along with what is arisen from what is mixed together with the mind. ^1-1212

1213. Which things are internal? Eye base…pe… mind base — these things are internal. ^1-1213

1214. Which things are external? Visible-form base…pe… mental-object base — these things are external. ^1-1214

1215. Which things are derived? Eye base…pe… nutriment taken in lumps — these things are derived. ^1-1215

1216. Which things are not derived? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, aggregate of consciousness, the four great elements, the unconditioned element — these things are not derived. ^1-1216

1217. Which things are grasped? Results of defiled things good for the mind and bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; whatever materiality is produced by action — these things are grasped. ^1-1217

1218. Which things are ungrasped? Things good for the mind, bad for the mind, defiled at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; things that are functional, neither good for the mind nor bad for the mind, nor results of action; whatever materiality is not produced by action; supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things are ungrasped. ^1-1218

##### Group on Holding On Tightly ^1-3-2-11-0

1219. Which things are ways of holding on tightly? Four ways of holding on tightly — grasping through sense desire, holding on tightly through views, holding on tightly through virtue and observances, grasping through personality view. ^1-1219

1220. What is grasping through sense desire? Whatever desire for sense pleasures, passion for sense pleasures, delight in sense pleasures, craving for sense pleasures, affection for sense pleasures, intensity for sense pleasures, confusion for sense pleasures, clinging to sense pleasures — this is called grasping through sense desire. ^1-1220

1221. What is holding on tightly through views? "There is no giving, no sacrifice, no offering, no fruit or result of good and bad action, no this world, no other world, no mother, no father, no beings reborn spontaneously, no ascetics and brahmins in the world who have gone rightly and practice rightly and who declare this world and the other world after realizing them by their own direct knowledge" — whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view, deep view, view as a thorn, view as distortion, view as agitation, thing that ties the mind down — view, grasping, taking up, settling into, holding to, wrong path, wrong way, wrongness, sectarian standpoint, upside-down grasping — this is called holding on tightly through views. Except holding on tightly through virtue and observances and grasping through personality view, all wrong view is holding on tightly through views. ^1-1221

1222. What is holding on tightly through virtue and observances? The view of outside ascetics and brahmins that "purity comes from virtue, purity comes from observance, purity comes from virtue and observance"; whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view, deep view, view as a thorn, view as distortion, view as agitation, thing that ties the mind down — view, grasping, taking up, settling into, holding to, wrong path, wrong way, wrongness, sectarian standpoint, upside-down grasping — this is called holding on tightly through virtue and observances. ^1-1222

1223. What is grasping through personality view? Here an untaught ordinary person who has not seen the noble ones, is unskilled and untrained in the noble teaching, has not seen good persons, is unskilled and untrained in the teaching of good persons — regards form as a self, or a self as possessing form, or form as in a self, or a self as in form; regards feeling…pe… perceiving…pe… habits…pe… consciousness as a self, or a self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in a self, or a self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view, deep view, view as a thorn, view as distortion, view as agitation, thing that ties the mind down — view, grasping, taking up, settling into, holding to, wrong path, wrong way, wrongness, sectarian standpoint, upside-down grasping — this is called grasping through personality view.
These things are graspings. ^1-1223

1224. Which things are not ways of holding on tightly? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not ways of holding on tightly. ^1-1224

1225. Which things can be grasped? Things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind defiled at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can be grasped. ^1-1225

1226. Which things cannot be grasped? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things cannot be grasped. ^1-1226

1227. Which things are associated with holding on tightly? Whatever things are associated with those graspings — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with holding on tightly. ^1-1227

1228. Which things are dissociated from holding on tightly? Whatever things are dissociated from those graspings — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from holding on tightly. ^1-1228

1229. Which things are both ways of holding on tightly and can be grasped? Those very ways of holding on tightly are both ways of holding on tightly and can be grasped. ^1-1229

1230. Which things can be grasped but are not graspings? Except those ways of holding on tightly, the remaining defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can be grasped but are not graspings. ^1-1230

1231. Which things are both ways of holding on tightly and associated with holding on tightly? Grasping through views is both grasping and associated with holding on tightly through sense desire as grasping; grasping through sense desire is both grasping and associated with holding on tightly through views as grasping; holding on tightly through virtue and observances is both grasping and associated with holding on tightly through sense desire as grasping; grasping through sense desire is both grasping and associated with holding on tightly through virtue and observances as grasping; grasping through personality view is both grasping and associated with holding on tightly through sense desire as grasping; grasping through sense desire is both grasping and associated with holding on tightly through personality view as grasping — these things are both ways of holding on tightly and associated with holding on tightly. ^1-1231

1232. Which things are associated with holding on tightly but are not graspings? Except those ways of holding on tightly, aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with holding on tightly but are not graspings. ^1-1232

1233. Which things are dissociated from holding on tightly but can be grasped? Whatever things are dissociated from those graspings but can be grasped — defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are dissociated from holding on tightly but can be grasped. ^1-1233

1234. Which things are dissociated from holding on tightly and cannot be grasped? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from holding on tightly and cannot be grasped.
Second Recitation Section in the Book on Summary. ^1-1234

##### Group on Harmful Habits of Mind ^1-3-2-12-0

1235. Which things are harmful habits of mind? Ten bases of harmful habit of mind — greed, hatred, delusion, conceit, view, doubt, stiffness, restlessness, lack of conscience, lack of concern about wrongdoing. ^1-1235

1236. What is greed? Whatever is passion, being passionate, inclination, favoring, delight, delight in passion, passion of mind, desire, confusion, clinging, greed, thick greed, attachment, mud, stickiness, fever, deceit, what gives birth, what ties, what weaves, what nets, what flows, what sticks, what spreads, what pulls along, second wish, plan, stream of becoming, forest, thicket, entanglement, affection, expectation, bond, hope, wishing, state of wishing, hope for visible form, hope for sound, hope for smell, hope for taste, hope for touch, hope for gain, hope for wealth, hope for children, hope for life, muttering, repeated muttering, intense muttering, muttering, state of muttering, greediness, being greedy, state of being greedy, being bent on gain, wanting what is good improperly, wrong passion, uneven greed, bending toward, wishing, longing, aspiring, craving for sense pleasures, craving for becoming, craving for non-becoming, craving for visible form, craving for sound, craving for smell, craving for taste, craving for touch, craving for mental objects, flood, yoke, knot, grasping, obstruction, hindrance, covering, bond, stain, underlying tendency, outbreak, creeper, dissatisfaction, root of suffering, source of suffering, origin of suffering, snare of Māra, bait of Māra, domain of Māra, river of craving, net of craving, rope of craving, ocean of craving, coveting, greed as a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called greed. ^1-1236

1237. What is hatred? "He did me wrong" — hatred arises; "he does me wrong" — hatred arises; "he will do me wrong" — hatred arises; "he did wrong to what is dear and pleasing to me"…pe… "he does wrong"…pe… "he will do wrong" — hatred arises; "he did good to what is disliked and displeasing to me"…pe… "he does good"…pe… "he will do good" — hatred arises; or hatred arises where there is no reason. Whatever hatred of this kind in the mind — hatred, resistance, opposing, anger, angry state, full anger, hatred, hateful state, full hatred, crookedness of mind, ill-will of mind, hatred, anger, being angry, state of being angry, hatred, hating, state of hating, ill-will, injuring, state of injuring, opposition, resisting, fierceness, being like an enemy, displeasure of mind — this is called hatred. ^1-1237

1238. What is delusion? Not knowing suffering, not knowing the origin of suffering, not knowing the cessation of suffering, not knowing the way leading to the cessation of suffering, not knowing the past, not knowing the future, not knowing past and future together, not knowing things that arise dependently with conditions as basis; whatever not-knowing of this kind — ignorance, not seeing, not understanding, not penetrating, not realizing, not taking in, not going all the way through, not looking at, not reviewing, not looking back, dullness, foolishness, lack of clear comprehension, delusion, full delusion, complete delusion, ignorance, flood of ignorance, yoke of ignorance, underlying tendency of ignorance, outbreak of ignorance, banner of ignorance, delusion as a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called delusion. ^1-1238

1239. What is conceit? The conceit "I am superior," the conceit "I am equal," the conceit "I am inferior"; whatever conceit of this kind — conceiving, state of conceiving, uplift, upliftedness, banner, taking hold, desire to be chief in the mind — this is called conceit. ^1-1239

1240. What is view? "The world is eternal," or "the world is not eternal," or "the world is finite," or "the world is infinite," or "the soul is the same as the body," or "the soul is one thing and the body another," or "the Tathāgata exists after death," or "the Tathāgata does not exist after death," or "the Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death," or "the Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death"; whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view, deep view, view as a thorn, view as distortion, view as agitation, thing that ties the mind down — view, grasping, taking up, settling into, holding to, wrong path, wrong way, wrongness, sectarian standpoint, upside-down grasping — this is called view. All wrong view is view. ^1-1240

1241. What is doubt? Doubt and wavering about the Teacher, about the teaching, about the community, about the training, about the past, about the future, about past and future together, about things that arise dependently with conditions as basis. Whatever wavering of this kind — doubting, state of doubting, confusion, doubt, being of two minds, double path, uncertainty, not taking one side, hesitation, trembling, not going all the way through, stiffness of mind, crookedness of mind — this is called doubt. ^1-1241

1242. What is stiffness? Whatever unfitness and unworkability of mind, sinking, settling, being sunk, settling, settled state, stiffness, stiffening, stiff state of mind — this is called stiffness. ^1-1242

1243. What is restlessness? Whatever restlessness, unsettledness, scattering, scattered state of mind — this is called restlessness. ^1-1243

1244. What is lack of conscience? Whatever does not feel shame at what should be felt shame at, does not feel shame at reaching bad things bad for the mind — this is called lack of conscience. ^1-1244

1245. What is lack of concern about wrongdoing? Whatever does not feel concern at what should be felt concern at, does not feel concern at reaching bad things bad for the mind — this is called lack of concern about wrongdoing.
These things are harmful habits of mind. ^1-1245

1246. Which things are not harmful habits of mind? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not harmful habits of mind. ^1-1246

1247. Which things can stain? Things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind defiled at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can stain. ^1-1247

1248. Which things cannot stain? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things cannot stain. ^1-1248

1249. Which things are stained? The three roots of what is bad for the mind — greed, hatred, delusion; harmful habits of mind of the same kind; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action arising from them — these things are stained. ^1-1249

1250. Which things are unstained? Things good for the mind and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are unstained. ^1-1250

1251. Which things are associated with harmful habits of mind? Whatever things are associated with those harmful habits of mind — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with harmful habits of mind. ^1-1251

1252. Which things are dissociated from harmful habits of mind? Whatever things are dissociated from those harmful habits of mind — aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from harmful habits of mind. ^1-1252

1253. Which things are both harmful habits of mind and can stain? Those very harmful habits of mind are both harmful habits of mind and can stain. ^1-1253

1254. Which things can stain but are not harmful habits of mind? Except those harmful habits of mind, the remaining defiled things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things can stain but are not harmful habits of mind. ^1-1254

1255. Which things are both harmful habits of mind and stained? Those very harmful habits of mind are both harmful habits of mind and stained. ^1-1255

1256. Which things are stained but are not harmful habits of mind? Except those harmful habits of mind, aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are stained but are not harmful habits of mind. ^1-1256

1257. Which things are both harmful habits of mind and associated with harmful habits of mind? Greed is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with greed as a harmful habit of mind; hatred is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with hatred as a harmful habit of mind; conceit is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with conceit as a harmful habit of mind; view is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with view as a harmful habit of mind; doubt is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with doubt as a harmful habit of mind; stiffness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with stiffness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with delusion as a harmful habit of mind; delusion is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; greed is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with greed as a harmful habit of mind; hatred is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with hatred as a harmful habit of mind; conceit is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with conceit as a harmful habit of mind; view is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with view as a harmful habit of mind; doubt is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with doubt as a harmful habit of mind; stiffness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with stiffness as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with restlessness as a harmful habit of mind; restlessness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; greed is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with greed as a harmful habit of mind; hatred is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with hatred as a harmful habit of mind; conceit is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with conceit as a harmful habit of mind; view is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with view as a harmful habit of mind; doubt is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with doubt as a harmful habit of mind; stiffness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with stiffness as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; greed is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with greed as a harmful habit of mind; hatred is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with hatred as a harmful habit of mind; conceit is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with conceit as a harmful habit of mind; view is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with view as a harmful habit of mind; doubt is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with doubt as a harmful habit of mind; stiffness is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with stiffness as a harmful habit of mind; lack of conscience is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of concern about wrongdoing as a harmful habit of mind; lack of concern about wrongdoing is both a harmful habit of mind and associated with lack of conscience as a harmful habit of mind — these things are both harmful habits of mind and associated with harmful habits of mind. ^1-1257

1258. Which things are associated with harmful habits of mind but are not harmful habits of mind? Except those harmful habits of mind, aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are associated with harmful habits of mind but are not harmful habits of mind. ^1-1258

1259. Which things are dissociated from harmful habits of mind but can stain? Whatever things are dissociated from those harmful habits of mind but can stain — defiled things good for the mind and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are dissociated from harmful habits of mind but can stain. ^1-1259

1260. Which things are dissociated from harmful habits of mind and cannot stain? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things are dissociated from harmful habits of mind and cannot stain. ^1-1260

##### Rear Pairs ^1-3-2-13-0

1261. Which things are to be removed by insight? The three things that tie the mind down — personality view, doubt, taking virtue and observances as sufficient. ^1-1261

1262. What is personality view? Here an untaught ordinary person who has not seen the noble ones, is unskilled and untrained in the noble teaching, has not seen good persons, is unskilled and untrained in the teaching of good persons — regards form as a self, or a self as possessing form, or form as in a self, or a self as in form; regards feeling…pe… perceiving…pe… habits…pe… consciousness as a self, or a self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in a self, or a self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called personality view. ^1-1262

1263. What is doubt? Doubt and wavering about the Teacher…pe… stiffness of mind, crookedness of mind — this is called doubt. ^1-1263

1264. What is taking virtue and observances as sufficient? The view of outside ascetics and brahmins that "purity comes from virtue, purity comes from observance, purity comes from virtue and observance"; whatever view of this kind…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called taking virtue and observances as sufficient. These three things that tie the mind down; harmful habits of mind of the same kind; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action arising from them — these things are to be removed by insight. ^1-1264

1265. Which things are not to be removed by insight? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not to be removed by insight. ^1-1265

1266. Which things are to be removed by developing? The remaining greed, hatred, and delusion; harmful habits of mind of the same kind; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action arising from them — these things are to be removed by developing. ^1-1266

1267. Which things are not to be removed by developing? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not to be removed by developing. ^1-1267

1268. Which things have causes that are to be removed by insight? The three things that tie the mind down — personality view, doubt, taking virtue and observances as sufficient. ^1-1268

1269. What is personality view? Here an untaught ordinary person who has not seen the noble ones, is unskilled and untrained in the noble teaching, has not seen good persons, is unskilled and untrained in the teaching of good persons — regards form as a self, or a self as possessing form, or form as in a self, or a self as in form; regards feeling…pe… perceiving…pe… habits…pe… consciousness as a self, or a self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in a self, or a self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called personality view. ^1-1269

1270. What is doubt? Doubt and wavering about the Teacher…pe… stiffness of mind, crookedness of mind — this is called doubt. ^1-1270

1271. What is taking virtue and observances as sufficient? The view of outside ascetics and brahmins that "purity comes from virtue, purity comes from observance, purity comes from virtue and observance"; whatever view of this kind…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called taking virtue and observances as sufficient. These three things that tie the mind down; harmful habits of mind of the same kind; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action arising from them — these things have causes that are to be removed by insight. The three things that tie the mind down — personality view, doubt, taking virtue and observances as sufficient — these things are to be removed by insight. Greed, hatred, and delusion of the same kind — these things are causes that are to be removed by insight. Harmful habits of mind of the same kind; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action arising from them — these things have causes that are to be removed by insight. ^1-1271

1272. Which things have causes that are not to be removed by insight? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things have causes that are not to be removed by insight. ^1-1272

1273. Which things have causes that are to be removed by developing? The remaining greed, hatred, and delusion — these things are causes that are to be removed by developing. Harmful habits of mind of the same kind; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action arising from them — these things have causes that are to be removed by developing. ^1-1273

1274. Which things have causes that are not to be removed by developing? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things have causes that are not to be removed by developing. ^1-1274

1275. Which things have putting the mind onto something? At the sense-desire level, form level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere of putting the mind onto something, except putting the mind onto something itself; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them — these things have putting the mind onto something. ^1-1275

1276. Which things are without putting the mind onto something? At the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere without putting the mind onto something; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; putting the mind onto something itself, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are without putting the mind onto something. ^1-1276

1277. Which things have keeping the mind on something? At the sense-desire level, form level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere of keeping the mind on something, except keeping the mind on something itself; the aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, aggregate of consciousness associated with them — these things have keeping the mind on something. ^1-1277

1278. Which things are without keeping the mind on something? At the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere without keeping the mind on something; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; keeping the mind on something itself, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are without keeping the mind on something. ^1-1278

1279. Which things have joy? At the sense-desire level, form level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere of joy, except joy itself; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them — these things have joy. ^1-1279

1280. Which things are without joy? At the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere without joy; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; joy itself, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are without joy. ^1-1280

1281. Which things are accompanied by a feeling of joy? At the sense-desire level, form level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere of joy, except joy itself; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them — these things are accompanied by a feeling of joy. ^1-1281

1282. Which things are not accompanied by a feeling of joy? Not in the sphere of joy at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; joy itself, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not accompanied by a feeling of joy. ^1-1282

1283. Which things are accompanied by ease? At the sense-desire level, form level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere of ease, except ease itself; the aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, aggregate of consciousness associated with them — these things are accompanied by ease. ^1-1283

1284. Which things are not accompanied by ease? Not in the sphere of ease at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; ease itself, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not accompanied by ease. ^1-1284

1285. Which things are accompanied by calm balance? At the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level in the sphere of calm balance, except calm balance itself; the aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, aggregate of consciousness associated with them — these things are accompanied by calm balance. ^1-1285

1286. Which things are not accompanied by calm balance? Not in the sphere of calm balance at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; calm balance itself, all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not accompanied by calm balance. ^1-1286

1287. Which things are of the sense-desire level? Making the Avīci hell the lower limit and the Paranimmitavasavattī devas the upper limit, whatever aggregates, elements, and sense bases belonging here, included here — materiality, feeling, perceiving, habits, consciousness — these things are of the sense-desire level. ^1-1287

1288. Which things are not of the sense-desire level? Form level, formless level, supra-mundane — these things are not of the sense-desire level. ^1-1288

1289. Which things are of the form level? Making the Brahmā world the lower limit and the Akaniṭṭha devas the upper limit, whatever mind and mental qualities belonging here, included here, of one who has attained absorption, or is reborn there, or abides in happiness visible here and now — these things are of the form level. ^1-1289

1290. Which things are not of the form level? Sense-desire level, formless level, supra-mundane — these things are not of the form level. ^1-1290

1291. Which things are of the formless level? Making the devas who have attained the base of infinite space the lower limit and the devas who have attained the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception the upper limit, whatever mind and mental qualities belonging here, included here, of one who has attained absorption, or is reborn there, or abides in happiness visible here and now — these things are of the formless level. ^1-1291

1292. Which things are not of the formless level? Sense-desire level, form level, supra-mundane — these things are not of the formless level. ^1-1292

1293. Which things are included? Things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind defiled at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are included. ^1-1293

1294. Which things are not included? Paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things are not included. ^1-1294

1295. Which things lead out? The four supra-mundane paths — these things lead out. ^1-1295

1296. Which things do not lead out? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things do not lead out. ^1-1296

1297. Which things are fixed? The five actions with immediate result, fixed wrong view, and the four supra-mundane paths — these things are fixed. ^1-1297

1298. Which things are not fixed? Except those things, the remaining things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are not fixed. ^1-1298

1299. Which things are inferior? Things good for the mind, bad for the mind, and neither good nor bad for the mind defiled at the sense-desire level, form level, and formless level; aggregate of materiality…pe… aggregate of consciousness — these things are inferior. ^1-1299

1300. Which things are unsurpassed? Supra-mundane paths and path results; the unconditioned element — these things are unsurpassed. ^1-1300

1301. Which things are with a refuge? The three roots of what is bad for the mind — greed, hatred, delusion; harmful habits of mind of the same kind; the aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness associated with them; bodily action, verbal action, and mental action arising from them — these things are with a refuge. ^1-1301

1302. Which things are without a refuge? Things good for the mind and neither good nor bad for the mind at the sense-desire level, form level, formless level, and supra-mundane level; aggregate of feeling…pe… aggregate of consciousness; all materiality, the unconditioned element — these things are without a refuge.
Abhidhamma Pairs. ^1-1302

#### Discourse Pair Summary ^1-3-3-0

1303. Which things share in knowledge? Things associated with knowledge — these things share in knowledge. ^1-1303

1304. Which things share in ignorance? Things associated with ignorance — these things share in ignorance. ^1-1304

1305. Which things are like a flash of wisdom? Wisdom in the three lower noble paths — these things are like a flash of wisdom. ^1-1305

1306. Which things are like a hard diamond? Wisdom in the highest path to arahantship — these things are like a hard diamond. ^1-1306

1307. Which things are foolish? Lack of conscience and lack of concern about wrongdoing — these things are foolish. All things bad for the mind are foolish. ^1-1307

1308. Which things are wise? Conscience and concern about wrongdoing — these things are wise. All things good for the mind are wise. ^1-1308

1309. Which things are dark? Lack of conscience and lack of concern about wrongdoing — these things are dark. All things bad for the mind are dark. ^1-1309

1310. Which things are bright? Conscience and concern about wrongdoing — these things are bright. All things good for the mind are bright. ^1-1310

1311. Which things bring blame? Bad bodily conduct, bad verbal conduct, bad mental conduct — these things bring blame. All things bad for the mind bring blame. ^1-1311

1312. Which things do not bring blame? Good bodily conduct, good verbal conduct, good mental conduct — these things do not bring blame. All things good for the mind do not bring blame. ^1-1312

1313. Which things are names for other things? Whatever counting, common label, designation, expression, name, naming, named thing, verbal expression, wording, and speech there is for those various things — these things are names for other things. All things are ways of naming other things. ^1-1313

1314. Which things are about how words are formed? Whatever counting, common label, designation, expression, name, naming, named thing, verbal expression, wording, and speech there is for those various things — these things are about how words are formed. All things are ways words are formed. ^1-1314

1315. Which things are designations? Whatever counting, common label, designation, expression, name, naming, named thing, verbal expression, wording, and speech there is for those various things — these things are designations. All things are ways of designating. ^1-1315

1316. Therein, what is name? Aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, group of the mind's habits and intentions that shape actions, aggregate of consciousness, and the unconditioned element — this is called name. ^1-1316

1317. Therein, what is form? The four great elements and materiality derived from the four great elements — this is called form. ^1-1317

1318. Therein, what is ignorance? Not knowing suffering, not seeing…pe… ignorance as a limb, delusion as a root of what is bad for the mind — this is called ignorance. ^1-1318

1319. Therein, what is craving for becoming? Whatever desire for existence, desire for becoming…pe… clinging to becoming — this is called craving for becoming. ^1-1319

1320. Therein, what is the view that existence continues? "There will be a self and a world," whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called the view that existence continues. ^1-1320

1321. Therein, what is the view that existence ends completely? "There will not be a self and a world," whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called the view that existence ends completely. ^1-1321

1322. Therein, what is the view that things last forever? "A self and a world are eternal," whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called the view that things last forever. ^1-1322

1323. Therein, what is the view that things are completely destroyed? "A self and a world will be cut off," whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called the view that things are completely destroyed. ^1-1323

1324. Therein, what is the view that things have limits? "A self and a world are limited," whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called the view that things have limits. ^1-1324

1325. Therein, what is the view that things have no limits? "A self and a world are unlimited," whatever view of this kind — view, fixed view…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called the view that things have no limits. ^1-1325

1326. Therein, what are views about the far past? Whatever view arises with regard to the far past — view, fixed view…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called views about the far past. ^1-1326

1327. Therein, what are views about the far future? Whatever view arises with regard to the far future — view, fixed view…pe… upside-down grasping — this is called views about the far future. ^1-1327

1328. Therein, what is lack of conscience? Whatever does not feel shame at what should be felt shame at, does not feel shame at reaching bad things bad for the mind — this is called lack of conscience. ^1-1328

1329. Therein, what is lack of concern about wrongdoing? Whatever does not feel concern at what should be felt concern at, does not feel concern at reaching bad things bad for the mind — this is called lack of concern about wrongdoing. ^1-1329

1330. Therein, what is conscience? Whatever feels shame at what should be felt shame at, feels shame at reaching bad things bad for the mind — this is called conscience. ^1-1330

1331. Therein, what is concern about wrongdoing? Whatever feels concern at what should be felt concern at, feels concern at reaching bad things bad for the mind — this is called concern about wrongdoing. ^1-1331

1332. Therein, what is being hard to advise? When spoken to by one who shares the teaching, being hard to advise, state of being hard to advise, taking what is disagreeable, being contrary, disrespect, state of disrespect, lack of respect, lack of deference — this is called being hard to advise. ^1-1332

1333. Therein, what is having bad friends? Those persons who are without faith, without moral conduct, with little learning, stingy, with little wisdom; whatever associating with them, following them, keeping company with them, joining with them, mixing with them, devotion, shared devotion, and going along with them — this is called having bad friends. ^1-1333

1334. Therein, what is being easy to advise? When spoken to by one who shares the teaching, being easy to advise, state of being easy to advise, not taking what is disagreeable, not being contrary, respect, state of respect, having respect, having deference — this is called being easy to advise. ^1-1334

1335. Therein, what is having good friends? Those persons who have faith, moral conduct, much learning, generosity, wisdom; whatever associating with them, following them, keeping company with them, joining with them, mixing with them, devotion, shared devotion, and going along with them — this is called having good friends. ^1-1335

1336. Therein, what is skill in falling into faults? The five groups of faults, the seven groups of faults. Whatever wisdom in knowing those faults — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill in falling into faults. ^1-1336

1337. Therein, what is skill in rising from faults? Whatever wisdom in rising from those faults — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill in rising from faults. ^1-1337

1338. Therein, what is skill in entering deep calm? There is attainment with putting the mind onto something and keeping the mind on something; there is attainment with only keeping the mind on something; there is attainment without putting the mind onto something or keeping the mind on something. Whatever wisdom in knowing those attainments — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill in entering deep calm. ^1-1338

1339. Therein, what is skill in rising from deep calm? Whatever wisdom in rising from those attainments — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill in rising from deep calm. ^1-1339

1340. Therein, what is skill regarding elements? The eighteen elements — eye element, visible-form element, eye-consciousness element; ear element, sound element, ear-consciousness element; nose element, smell element, nose-consciousness element; tongue element, taste element, tongue-consciousness element; body element, touch element, body-consciousness element; mind element, mental-object element, mind-consciousness element. Whatever wisdom in knowing those elements — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill regarding elements. ^1-1340

1341. Therein, what is skill in paying attention? Whatever wisdom in paying attention to those elements — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill in paying attention. ^1-1341

1342. Therein, what is skill regarding sense bases? The twelve sense bases — eye base, visible-form base, ear base, sound base, nose base, smell base, tongue base, taste base, body base, touch base, mind base, mental-object base. Whatever wisdom in knowing those sense bases — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill regarding sense bases. ^1-1342

1343. Therein, what is skill regarding how things arise together? With ignorance as condition, habits; with habits as condition, consciousness; with consciousness as condition, name-and-form; with name-and-form as condition, the six sense bases; with the six sense bases as condition, contact; with contact as condition, feeling; with feeling as condition, craving; with craving as condition, grasping; with grasping as condition, becoming; with becoming as condition, birth; with birth as condition, aging-and-death, sorrow, lamentation, pain, displeasure, and despair come to be; thus is the arising of this whole mass of suffering. Whatever wisdom in knowing that — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill regarding how things arise together. ^1-1343

1344. Therein, what is skill in what is possible? Whatever things are causes and conditions for the arising of those various things as possible, whatever wisdom in knowing that — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill in what is possible. ^1-1344

1345. Therein, what is skill in what is impossible? Whatever things are not causes and not conditions for the arising of those various things as impossible, whatever wisdom in knowing that — knowing, understanding…pe… non-delusion, examining things, right view — this is called skill in what is impossible. ^1-1345

1346. Therein, what is uprightness? Whatever uprightness, straightness, not crooked, not bent — this is called uprightness. ^1-1346

1347. Therein, what is gentleness? Whatever softness, gentleness, not harsh, not hard, lowliness of mind — this is called gentleness. ^1-1347

1348. Therein, what is patience? Whatever patience, bearing, enduring, not fierce, not rough, contentment of mind — this is called patience. ^1-1348

1349. Therein, what is mildness? Bodily non-transgression, verbal non-transgression, bodily-and-verbal non-transgression — this is called mildness. All restraint of moral conduct is mildness. ^1-1349

1350. Therein, what is friendliness? Having given up speech that is rough, harsh, hurtful to others, connected with anger, not leading to calm, and having spoken speech that is smooth, pleasant to the ear, loving, going to the heart, polite, pleasing to many, pleasing to many people; whatever gentleness of speech, kind speech, non-harsh speech there is — this is called friendliness. ^1-1350

1351. Therein, what is hospitality? Two kinds of hospitality — hospitality with material things and hospitality with the teaching. Here someone is hospitable with hospitality through material things or through the teaching — this is called hospitality. ^1-1351

1352. Therein, what is not guarding the sense doors? Here someone, on seeing a form with the eye, grasps at signs and grasps at details. As to that eye faculty dwelling unguarded, bad things bad for the mind of coveting and displeasure might flow in; he does not practice restraint, does not guard the eye faculty, does not undertake restraint in the eye faculty. On hearing a sound with the ear…pe… on smelling a smell with the nose…pe… on tasting a taste with the tongue…pe… on touching a touch with the body…pe… on cognizing a mental object with the mind, grasps at signs and grasps at details. As to that mind faculty dwelling unguarded, bad things bad for the mind of coveting and displeasure might flow in; he does not practice restraint, does not guard the mind faculty, does not undertake restraint in the mind faculty. Whatever non-guarding, non-protection, non-keeping, non-restraint of these six faculties — this is called not guarding the sense doors. ^1-1352

1353. Therein, what is ignorance the right amount of food? Here someone takes food without reflection and unwisely — for fun, for intoxication, for making beautiful, for ornamentation. Whatever discontent, ignorance the right amount, lack of reflection in food — this is called ignorance the right amount of food. ^1-1353

1354. Therein, what is guarding the sense doors? Here someone, on seeing a form with the eye, does not grasp at signs and does not grasp at details. As to that eye faculty dwelling unguarded, bad things bad for the mind of coveting and displeasure might flow in; he practices restraint, guards the eye faculty, undertakes restraint in the eye faculty. On hearing a sound with the ear…pe… on smelling a smell with the nose…pe… on tasting a taste with the tongue…pe… on touching a touch with the body…pe… on cognizing a mental object with the mind, does not grasp at signs and does not grasp at details.
